This shows (
ii
).

We show (
i
).
By
Lemma 18
,
\rho^{*}
is positive. Then
F^{*}(\rho^{*})=U\rho^{*}U^{\dagger}
is positive.
By (
ii
),
F^{*}(\rho^{*})
is trace-class and
\operatorname{tr}F^{*}(\rho^{*})=\operatorname{tr}\rho_{1}\cdots\operatorname{tr}\rho_{n}
.
Thus
\operatorname{tr}F^{*}(\rho^{*})=1
(or
\leq 1
) if
\operatorname{tr}\rho_{i}=1
(or
\leq 1
) for
i=1,\dots,n
.
By definition of (sub)density operators, this shows (
i
).

We show (
iii
).
It is sufficient to consider the case
\pi
is a transposition of neighboring values since all permutations are generated from these.
That is,
\pi(k)=k+1
and
\pi(k+1)=k
for some fixed
k\in\{{1,\dots,n-1}\}
and
\pi=\mathit{id}
everywhere else.
Let
F^{\pi}:=({F_{\pi(1)},\dots,F_{\pi(n)}})
and
\rho^{\pi}:=\rho_{\pi(1)}\otimes\dots\otimes\rho_{\pi(n)}

That is, we need to show
F^{*}(\rho^{*})=F^{\pi}(\rho^{\pi})
.
By Laws
2
–
2
,
2
,
F^{\pi}=F^{*}.\Pi
for
\Pi:=\mathit{id}\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}\dots\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}\mathit{id}\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}(\alpha\circ(\sigma\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}\mathit{id})\circ\alpha^{\prime})
.
(Recall that
\otimes
is right-associative.)
And by Laws
,
2
,
2
,
2
,
\rho^{\pi}=\Pi^{\prime}(\rho^{*})
for
\Pi^{\prime}:=\mathit{id}\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}\dots\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}\mathit{id}\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}(\alpha\circ(\sigma\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}\mathit{id})\circ\alpha^{\prime})
.
(We use different variable names for
\Pi
and
\Pi^{\prime}
even though their definitions seem identical because they have different types.)
Furthermore, since
\sigma
is self-inverse, and
\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
are inverses,
\Pi\circ\Pi^{\prime}=\mathit{id}
.
Thus
F^{\pi}(\rho^{\pi})=F^{*}(\Pi\circ\Pi^{\prime}(\rho^{*}))=F^{*}(\rho^{*})
.
This shows (
iii
).

We show (
iv
).
We have

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274ptF_{1}(U\rho_{1}V)\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{2}(\rho_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\rho_{n})

\displaystyle=F^{*}((U\rho_{1}V)\otimes\rho_{2}\otimes\dots\otimes\rho_{n})

\displaystyle=F^{*}\bigl({({U\otimes\mathit{id}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathit{id}})\rho^{*}({V\otimes\mathit{id}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathit{id}})}\bigr)

\displaystyle=F^{*}({U\otimes\mathit{id}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathit{id}})F^{*}(\rho^{*})F^{*}({V\otimes\mathit{id}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathit{id}})

\displaystyle=F_{1}({U})F^{*}(\rho^{*})F_{1}({V}).

This shows (
iv
).

We show (
v
).

Assume all
\rho_{i}
have rank 1.
An operator has rank 1 iff it is of the form
\psi\eta^{\dagger}
for nonzero vectors
\psi,\eta

[
Con13
, Exercise II.4.8]
.
Then
\rho_{i}=\psi_{i}\eta_{i}^{\dagger}
for nonzero
\psi_{i},\eta_{i}

and then
\rho^{*}=\psi\eta^{\dagger}
where
\psi:=\psi_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\psi_{n}
and
\eta:=\eta_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\eta_{n}
.
Note that
\psi,\eta
are non-zero.
Then
F^{*}(\rho^{*})=U\rho^{*}U^{\dagger}=(U\psi)(U\eta)^{\dagger}
.
Since
U
is unitary,
U\psi,U\eta
are non-zero.
Thus
F^{*}(\rho^{*})
is rank 1.

Now assume that at least one
\rho_{i}
does not have rank 1, say
\rho_{1}
.
And operator has rank 1 iff its image is one-dimensional
[
Con13
, Exercise II.4.8]
.
If any of the
\rho_{i}
is
0
, then
F^{*}(\rho^{*})=0
and thus does not have rank 1.
Since
\rho_{1}
does not have rank 1 and is non-zero, there exist
\eta_{1},\bar{\eta}_{1}
such that
\rho_{1}\eta_{1},\rho_{1}\bar{\eta}_{1}
are not collinear.
And since all
\rho_{i}
are non-zero, there exist
\eta_{i}
such that
\rho_{i}\eta_{i}
is non-zero.
Let
\eta:=\eta_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\eta_{n}
and
\bar{\eta}:=\bar{\eta}_{1}\otimes\eta_{2}\otimes\dots\otimes\eta_{n}
.
Then
\rho^{*}\eta,\rho^{*}\bar{\eta}
are not collinear.
We have
F^{*}(\rho^{*})U\eta=U\rho^{*}U^{\dagger}U\eta=U\rho^{*}\eta
and
F^{*}(\rho^{*})U\bar{\eta}=U\rho^{*}\bar{\eta}
.
Thus
U\rho^{*}\eta,U\rho^{*}\bar{\eta}
are in the image of
F^{*}(\rho^{*})
and not collinear.
Thus
F^{*}(\rho^{*})
does not have rank 1.

This shows (
v
).

We show (
vi
).
We only show bounded linearity in the first argument.
The general case follows with (
iii
).
Since the tensor-product of operators is bilinear,
\Phi
is linear in each argument.
Furthermore

\bigl\lVert{F^{*}(\rho_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\rho_{n})}\bigr\rVert_{\operatorname{tr}}=\bigl\lVert{U(\rho_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\rho_{n})U^{\dagger}}\bigr\rVert_{\operatorname{tr}}\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\lVert{\rho_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\rho_{n}}\rVert_{\operatorname{tr}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\lVert{\rho_{1}}\rVert_{\operatorname{tr}}\cdots\lVert{\rho_{n}}\rVert_{\operatorname{tr}}=\lVert{\rho_{1}}\rVert_{\operatorname{tr}}\cdot\prod_{i=2}^{n}\lVert{\rho_{n}}\rVert_{\operatorname{tr}}.

(19)

Here
(*)
is by
item i
, and
(**)
by
Lemma 32
.
Thus
\Phi
is bounded in its first argument (with respect to the trace-norm).
This shows (
vi
).

We show (
vii
).
Note that
({F_{1},F_{2}})
,
F_{3}
, …,
F_{n}
is a partition.

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt({F_{1},F_{2}})(\rho_{1}\otimes\rho_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{3}(\rho_{3})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\rho_{n})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny\eqref{lemma:mixed.states:permute}}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F_{3}(\rho_{3})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\rho_{n})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}({F_{1},F_{2}})(\rho_{1}\otimes\rho_{2})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F_{3}\dots F_{n}{F_{1}}{F_{2}}\bigl({\rho_{3}\otimes\dots\rho_{n}\otimes\rho_{1}\otimes\rho_{2}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F_{3}(\rho_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\rho_{n})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{1}(\rho_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{2}(\rho_{2})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny\eqref{lemma:mixed.states:permute}}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F_{1}(\rho_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{2}(\rho_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\rho_{n}).

Both occurrences of
(*)
are by definition of
\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}
(recall that
\otimes
and
({\cdot,\cdot})
are right-associative.
This shows (
vii
).

We show (
viii
).
By
Lemma 33
,
S:=\{\rho_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\rho_{n}:\rho_{i}\in S_{i}\}
generates
T(\mathcal{H})
with
\mathcal{H}:=\mathcal{H}_{A_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathcal{H}_{A_{n}}
.
Let
\operatorname{span}S
denote the linear span of
S
. That
S
generates
T(\mathcal{H})
means that
\operatorname{span}S
is dense in
T(\mathcal{H})
.
For
\rho\in T(\mathcal{H})
,

\displaystyle\bigl\lVert{F^{*}(\rho)}\bigr\rVert_{\operatorname{tr}}=\bigl\lVert{U\rho U^{\dagger}}\bigr\rVert_{\operatorname{tr}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{\leq}}\lVert{U}\rVert\cdot\lVert{\rho}\rVert_{\operatorname{tr}}\cdot\lVert{U^{\dagger}}\rVert=\lVert{\rho}\rVert_{\operatorname{tr}}.

Here
(*)
is by
item i
.
So
F^{*}
is bounded linear (w.r.t. the trace-norm). Hence
F^{*}
is continuous.
Hence
F^{*}(\operatorname{span}S)
is dense in
F^{*}(T(\mathcal{H}))
.
Since
F^{*}
is an iso-reference it is surjective.
Thus
F^{*}(T(\mathcal{H}))=T(\mathcal{H}_{B})
.
Since
F^{*}
is linear,
F^{*}(\operatorname{span}S)=\operatorname{span}{F^{*}(S)}
.
Thus
F^{*}(S)
generates
T(\mathcal{H}_{B})
.
This shows the first sentence of (
viii
).

The “in particular” part:
Since
\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F}
are linear and equal on
F^{*}(S)
, they are equal on
\operatorname{span}{F^{*}(S)}
.
Since
\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F}
are quantum subchannels, they are continuous with respect to the trace-norm (
Lemma 36
).
So since
\operatorname{span}{F^{*}(S)}
is dense in
T(\mathcal{H}_{B})
, we have that
\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F}
are equal on their whole domain
T(\mathcal{H}_{B})
.
This shows (
viii
).

We show (
ix
).
Let
G^{*}:=({G_{1},\dots,G_{m}})
. Then

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274ptF_{1}\Bigl({G_{1}(\sigma_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}G_{m}(\sigma_{m})}\Bigr)\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{2}(\rho_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\rho_{n})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny\eqref{lemma:mixed.states:permute}}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F_{2}(\rho_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\rho_{n})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{1}\Bigl({G_{1}(\sigma_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}G_{m}(\sigma_{m})}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}{F_{2}}\dots{F_{n}}{F_{1}}\Bigl({\rho_{2}\otimes\dots\otimes\rho_{n}\otimes G^{*}\bigl({\sigma_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\sigma_{m}}\bigr)}\Bigr)

\displaystyle={F_{2}}\dots{F_{n}}{F_{1}}\circ\Bigl({\mathit{id}\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}\dots\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}\mathit{id}\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G^{*}}\Bigr)\Bigl({\rho_{2}\otimes\dots\otimes\rho_{n}\otimes{\sigma_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\sigma_{m}}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({F_{2},\dots,F_{n},F_{1}.G^{*}})\Bigl({\rho_{2}\otimes\dots\otimes\rho_{n}\otimes{\sigma_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\sigma_{m}}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$\scriptscriptstyle(\!*\!*\!*\!)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({F_{2},\dots,F_{n},F_{1}.G_{1},\dots,F_{1}.G_{m}})\Bigl({\rho_{2}\otimes\dots\otimes\rho_{n}\otimes{\sigma_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\sigma_{m}}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F_{2}(\rho_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\rho_{n})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{1}.G_{1}(\sigma_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{1}.G_{m}(\sigma_{m})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny\eqref{lemma:mixed.states:permute}}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F_{1}.G_{1}(\sigma_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{1}.G_{m}(\sigma_{m})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{2}(\rho_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\rho_{n}).

Here
(*)
is by definition of
\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}
.
And
(**)
is by Law
2
.
And
(*{*}*)
uses
F_{1}.G^{*}={({F_{1}.G_{1},\dots,F_{1}.G_{m}})}
which follows from Law
2
.
Recall in this calculation that
\otimes
,
({\cdot,\cdot})
are right-associative.

This shows (
ix
).
∎

E.4
Pure states,
Lemma 6

Proof.

Throughout this proof, we abbreviate
\psi^{*}:=\psi_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\psi_{n}
and
\eta^{*}:=\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}
and
F^{*}:=({F_{1},\dots,F_{n}})
and
b:={F_{1}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}^{\dagger})}=F^{*}(\eta^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})
.
Note that
\eta^{*}=\eta_{\mathbf{A_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathbf{A_{n}}}
by definition of
\eta_{\dots}
.
Note that
F^{*}
is an iso-reference since
F_{1},\dots,F_{n}
is a partition by assumption.
And
{F_{1}(\rho_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{n}(\rho_{n})}=F^{*}(\psi^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})\xi_{b}
by definition.
Since
F^{*}
is an iso-reference, by
Lemma 26
,
F^{*}(\rho^{*})=U\rho^{*}U^{\dagger}
for some unitary
U
that we fix throughout the proof as well.

We show (
i
).

\bigl\lVert{F_{1}(\rho_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{n}(\rho_{n})}\bigr\rVert^{2}=\bigl\lVert{F^{*}(\psi^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})\xi_{b}}\bigr\rVert^{2}={\xi_{b}}^{\dagger}F^{*}(\psi^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})^{\dagger}F^{*}(\psi^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})\xi_{b}\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}{\xi_{b}}^{\dagger}F^{*}(\eta^{*}{\psi^{*}}^{\dagger}\psi^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})\xi_{b}=\lVert{\psi^{*}}\rVert^{2}\cdot{\xi_{b}}^{\dagger}F^{*}(\eta^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})\xi_{b}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\lVert{\psi^{*}}\rVert^{2}\cdot\lVert{\xi_{b}}\rVert^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$\scriptscriptstyle(\!*\!*\!*\!)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\lVert{\psi^{*}}\rVert^{2}\\
=\bigl({\lVert{\psi_{1}}\rVert\cdots\lVert{\psi_{n}}\rVert}\bigr)^{2}

Here
(*)
follows from the fact that references are multiplicative and preserve adjoints.
To see
(**)
, note that
\eta^{*}
is a unit vector, hence
\eta^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger}
is a projector, hence
b=F^{*}(\eta^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})
is a non-zero projector (
item iii
,
Lemma 25
).
And
\xi_{b}
is in the image of that projector by definition.
And
(*{*}*)
holds because
\xi_{b}
is a unit vector by definition.
This shows the first part of (
i
).
The “in particular” part follows because pure states are states of norm
1
.

We show (
ii
).

\displaystyle\hskip-10.00002ptF_{\pi(1)}(\psi_{\pi(1)})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{\pi(n)}(\psi_{\pi(n)})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\Bigl({F_{\pi(1)}(\psi_{\pi(1)}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{\pi(1)}})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{\pi(n)}(\psi_{\pi(n)}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{\pi(n)}})}\Bigr)\xi_{{F_{\pi(1)}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{\pi(1)}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{\pi(1)}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{\pi(n)}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{\pi(n)}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{\pi(n)}}^{\dagger})}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\Bigl({F_{1}(\psi_{1}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\psi_{n}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}})}\Bigr)\xi_{{F_{1}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}^{\dagger})}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F_{1}(\psi_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{2}(\psi_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{n}(\psi_{n}).

Here
(*)
is by definition of
\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}
and
(**)
by two applications of
item iii
.
This shows (
ii
).

We show (
iii
). Linearity is by construction, and boundedness follows from (
i
).

We show (
iv
).

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt({F_{1},F_{2}})(\psi_{1}\otimes\psi_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{3}(\psi_{3})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{n}(\psi_{n})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\Bigl({({F_{1},F_{2}})\bigl({({\psi_{1}\otimes\psi_{2}})\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\dagger}}\bigr)\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{3}(\psi_{3}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{3}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\psi_{n}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}^{\dagger})}\Bigr)\cdot{}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\xi_{{({F_{1},F_{2}})({\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{2}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\dagger}})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{3}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{3}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{3}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}^{\dagger})}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\Bigl({({F_{1},F_{2}})\bigl({\psi_{1}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes\psi_{2}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\dagger}}\bigr)\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{3}(\psi_{n}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{3}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\psi_{n}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}^{\dagger})}\Bigr)\cdot{}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\xi_{{({F_{1},F_{2}})({\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\dagger}})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{3}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{3}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{3}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}^{\dagger})}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$\scriptscriptstyle(\!*\!*\!*\!)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\Bigl({{F_{1}}\bigl({\psi_{1}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger}}\bigr)\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}{F_{2}}\bigl({\psi_{2}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\dagger}}\bigr)\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{3}(\psi_{n}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{3}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\psi_{n}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}^{\dagger})}\Bigr)\cdot{}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\xi_{{{F_{1}}({\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger}})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}{F_{2}}({\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\dagger}})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{3}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{3}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{3}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}^{\dagger})}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F_{1}(\psi_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{2}(\psi_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{n}(\psi_{n}).

Here
(*)
is by definition of
\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
And
(**)
follows since
\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}\otimes\mathbf{A}_{2}}=\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}
by definition.
And
(*{*}*)
is by
item vii
.
This shows (
iv
).

We show (
v
).
Let
S^{*}:=\bigl\{{\psi_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\psi_{n}:\forall i.\ \psi_{i}\in S_{i}}\bigr\}
.
By assumption,
S_{i}
generates
\mathcal{H}_{A_{i}}
, i.e.,
\overline{\operatorname{span}S_{i}}=\mathcal{H}_{A_{i}}
where
\overline{{}\cdot{}}
denotes the topological closure.
The algebraic tensor product of dense subspaces (such as
\operatorname{span}S_{i}
) of some Hilbert spaces is dense in the tensor product of those Hilbert spaces
[
Pru81
, Exercise II.6.9]
.
Thus
S^{*}
generates
\mathcal{H}^{*}:=\mathcal{H}_{A_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathcal{H}_{A_{n}}
, i.e.,
\overline{\operatorname{span}S^{*}}=\mathcal{H}^{*}
.
Let
S_{F}:=\bigl\{{F_{1}(\psi_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{2}(\psi_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{n}(\psi_{n}):\psi_{i}\in S_{i}}\bigr\}
.
We want to show that
S_{F}
generates
\mathcal{H}_{B}
.
We have

\displaystyle S_{F}

\displaystyle=\bigl\{{F^{*}\bigl({\psi_{1}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes\dots\otimes\psi_{n}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}^{\dagger}}\bigr)\xi_{b}:\psi_{i}\in S_{i}}\bigr\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\bigl\{{F^{*}\bigl({\psi{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger}}\bigr)\xi_{b}:\psi\in S^{*}}\bigr\}

\displaystyle=\bigl\{{U\psi{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger}U^{\dagger}\xi_{b}:\psi\in S^{*}}\bigr\}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\bigl\{{{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger}U^{\dagger}\xi_{b}\cdot U\psi:\psi\in S^{*}}\bigr\}

Here
(*)
follows by definition of
S^{*}
and the fact that
\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathbf{A}_{n}}=\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}
.
And for
(**)
notice that
{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger}U^{\dagger}\xi_{b}
is a scalar.
By definition,
\xi_{b}
is in the image of
b^{*}=F(\eta^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})=U\eta^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger}U^{\dagger}
.
In particular, it is not orthogonal to
\operatorname{im}U\eta^{*}
.
Thus
{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger}U^{\dagger}\xi_{b}\neq 0
.
Hence

\overline{\operatorname{span}S_{F}}=\overline{\operatorname{span}\bigl\{{{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger}U^{\dagger}\xi_{b}\cdot U\psi:\psi\in S^{*}}\bigr\}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\overline{\operatorname{span}\bigl\{{U\psi:\psi\in S^{*}}\bigr\}}=\overline{\operatorname{span}US^{*}}\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}U\,\overline{\operatorname{span}S^{*}}=U\mathcal{H}^{*}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\mathcal{H}_{B}.

Here
(*)
uses that
{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger}U^{\dagger}\xi_{b}
is a non-zero scalar factor and thus does not affect the span.
And
(**)
uses that
U:\mathcal{H}^{*}\to\mathcal{H}_{B}
is unitary.
Hence
S_{F}
generates
\mathcal{H}_{B}
.
This shows the first sentence of (
v
).
The “in particular” part follows because
U,V
are bounded operators and thus linear and continuous.

We show (
vii
).
By definition, a reference
F:\mathbf{A}\to\mathbf{B}
is
\eta
-regular if
({F,F^{\complement}})(\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes c)=\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}
for some
c
.
But since all complements of
F
are equivalent, it is sufficient to show that there is
some
complement
F^{\prime}
of
F
and some
c
such that
({F,F^{\prime}})(\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes c)=\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}
.
With that in mind, the fact that
\mathsf{Fst}:\mathbf{A}\to\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}
,
\mathsf{Snd}:\mathbf{B}\to\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}
,
\sigma:\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}\to\mathbf{B}\otimes\mathbf{A}
,
\alpha:(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B})\otimes\mathbf{C}\to\mathbf{A}\otimes(\mathbf{B}\otimes\mathbf{C})
,
\alpha^{\prime}
are
\eta
-regular is shown by the following calculations (always keeping in mind that
\eta_{\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}}=\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{B}}
):

\displaystyle({\mathsf{Fst},\mathsf{Snd}})(\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger})=(\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes 1)(1\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger})=\eta_{\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle({\mathsf{Snd},\mathsf{Fst}})(\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger})=(1\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger})(\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes 1)=\eta_{\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle({\sigma,\sigma^{\complement}})(\eta_{\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}\otimes 1)=({\sigma,\sigma^{\complement}})\bigl({(\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger})\otimes 1}\bigr)=\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}=\eta_{\mathbf{B}\otimes\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}\otimes\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} ({\alpha,\alpha^{\complement}})(\eta_{(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B})\otimes\mathbf{C}}\eta_{(\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B})\otimes\mathbf{C}}^{\dagger}\otimes 1)&=({\alpha,\alpha^{\complement}})\bigl({((\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger})\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}}^{\dagger})\otimes 1}\bigr)\\[-2.0pt]
&=\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}}^{\dagger})=\eta_{\mathbf{A}\otimes(\mathbf{B}\otimes\mathbf{C})}\eta_{\mathbf{A}\otimes(\mathbf{B}\otimes\mathbf{C})}^{\dagger}\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\alpha^{\prime}\text{ analogous}

This shows that
\mathsf{Fst},\mathsf{Snd},\sigma,\alpha,\alpha^{\prime}
are
\eta
-regular.

We next show that
FG
is
\eta
-regular for
\eta
-regular disjoint
F:\mathbf{A}\to\mathbf{C}
,
G:\mathbf{B}\to\mathbf{C}
.
By Law
4
,
({G,FG^{\complement}})
is a complement of
F
.
Then since
F
is
\eta
-regular, there exists a
c
such that
\bigl({F,({G,FG^{\complement}})}\bigr)(\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes c)=\eta_{\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}}^{\dagger}
.
Similarly, there exists a
d
such that
\bigl({G,({F,FG^{\complement}})}\bigr)(\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}\otimes d)=\eta_{\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}}^{\dagger}
.
Note that for all
a,b,z
,

\bigl({G,({F,FG^{\complement}})}\bigr)(b\otimes(a\otimes z))=\bigl({F,({G,FG^{\complement}})}\bigr)({a\otimes(b\otimes z)})

and thus, since
\bigl({F,({G,FG^{\complement}})}\bigr)
is an iso-reference,

\bigl({F,({G,FG^{\complement}})}\bigr)^{-1}\circ\bigl({G,({F,FG^{\complement}})}\bigr)(b\otimes(a\otimes z))=a\otimes(b\otimes z)
.
Thus

\underbrace{\sigma\circ\bigl({F,({G,FG^{\complement}})}\bigr)^{-1}\circ\bigl({G,({F,FG^{\complement}})}\bigr)\circ(1\otimes\sigma)}_{{}=:H}(b\otimes(z\otimes a))=(b\otimes z)\otimes a.

We also have

\alpha^{\prime}(b\otimes(z\otimes a))=(b\otimes z)\otimes a
.
By Law
2
, this implies that
H=\alpha^{\prime}
.
Thus

c\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}=\sigma(\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes c)=\sigma\circ\bigl({F,({G,FG^{\complement}})}\bigr)^{-1}(\eta_{\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}}^{\dagger})\\
=\sigma\circ\bigl({F,({G,FG^{\complement}})}\bigr)^{-1}\circ\bigl({G,({F,FG^{\complement}})}\bigr)(\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}\otimes d)=H(\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}\otimes\sigma(d))=\alpha^{\prime}(\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}\otimes\sigma(d)).

By
Lemma 21
, this implies that
c=\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}\otimes c^{\prime}
for some
c^{\prime}
.
(Using that
\alpha^{\prime}(x)=U_{\alpha}^{\dagger}xU_{\alpha}
for
U_{\alpha}
as in
Lemma 21
.)
Then we have

\bigl({FG,FG^{\complement}}\bigr)(\eta_{\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}\otimes c^{\prime})=\bigl({FG,FG^{\complement}}\bigr)\bigl({({\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}})\otimes c^{\prime}}\bigr)\\
=\bigl({F,({G,FG^{\complement}})}\bigr)\bigl({\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes(\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}\otimes c^{\prime})}\bigr)=\bigl({F,({G,FG^{\complement}})}\bigr)(\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes c)=\eta_{\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}}^{\dagger}.

This shows that
FG
is
\eta
-regular.

We next show that
F.G
is
\eta
-regular for
\eta
-regular
F:\mathbf{B}\to\mathbf{C}
,
G:\mathbf{A}\to\mathbf{B}
.
Since
G
is
\eta
-regular, there exists some
c
such that
({G,G^{\complement}})(\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes c)=\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}
.
Since
F
is
\eta
-regular, there exists some
d
such that
(F,F^{\complement})({\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}\otimes d})=\eta_{\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}}^{\dagger}
.
Thus

\bigl({F.G,({F^{\complement},F.G^{\complement}})}\bigr)\bigl({\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes(d\otimes c)}\bigr)=\bigl({F,F^{\complement}}\bigr)\bigl({({G,G^{\complement}})(\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes c)\otimes d}\bigr)=\bigl({F,F^{\complement}}\bigr)\bigl({\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}\otimes d}\bigr)=\eta_{\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}}^{\dagger}.

Since
{({F^{\complement},F.G^{\complement}})}
is a complement of
F.G
by Law
4
, this means that
F.G
is
\eta
-regular.

We next show that
F\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G
is
\eta
-regular for
\eta
-regular
F:\mathbf{A}\to\mathbf{C}
,
G:\mathbf{B}\to\mathbf{D}
.
By Law
4
,
F^{\complement}\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G^{\complement}
and
F\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G
are complements.
Thus by Law
4
,
F^{\complement}\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G^{\complement}
and
({F\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G})^{\complement}
are equivalent.
Thus by definition of equivalence, there exists an iso-reference
I
such that
F^{\complement}\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G^{\complement}=({F\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G})^{\complement}\circ I
.
Since
F,G
are
\eta
-regular, there exist
c_{F},c_{G}
such that
({F,F^{\complement}})(\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes c_{F})=\eta_{\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}}^{\dagger}
and
({G,G^{\complement}})(\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}\otimes c_{G})=\eta_{\mathbf{D}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}}^{\dagger}
.
Let
c:=I(c_{F}\otimes c_{G})
.
Then

\displaystyle\Bigl({F\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G,({F\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G})^{\complement}}\Bigr)\bigl({\eta_{\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}\otimes c}\bigr)=(F\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G)(\eta_{\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}\otimes\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger})\cdot{({F\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G})^{\complement}}(c)

\displaystyle=\bigl({F\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G}\bigr)\bigl({\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}}\bigr)\cdot{({F\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G})^{\complement}}\circ I(c_{F}\otimes c_{G})

\displaystyle=\bigl({F\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G}\bigr)\bigl({\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}}\bigr)\cdot\bigl({F^{\complement}\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G^{\complement}}\bigr)(c_{F}\otimes c_{G})=\Bigl({F(\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger})\otimes G(\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger})}\Bigr)\cdot\Bigl({F^{\complement}(c_{F})\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G^{\complement}(c_{G})}\Bigr)

\displaystyle={F(\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger})F^{\complement}(c_{F})\otimes G(\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger})G^{\complement}(c_{G})}=({F,F^{\complement}})({\eta_{\mathbf{A}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}}^{\dagger}\otimes c_{F}})\otimes({G,G^{\complement}})({\eta_{\mathbf{B}}\eta_{\mathbf{B}}^{\dagger}\otimes c_{G}})

\displaystyle=\eta_{\mathbf{C}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}}^{\dagger}\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{D}}\eta_{\mathbf{D}}^{\dagger}=\eta_{\mathbf{C}\otimes\mathbf{D}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}\otimes\mathbf{D}}^{\dagger}.

Thus
F\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G
is
\eta
-regular.

This shows (
vii
).

We show (
vi
).
Let
G^{*}:=({G_{1},\dots,G_{m}})
and
\xi_{G}^{*}:=\xi_{{G_{1}(\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{1}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}G_{m}(\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{m}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{m}}^{\dagger})}}

and
\mathbf{C}^{*}:=\mathbf{C}_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathbf{C}_{m}
and
\mathbf{A}^{*}:=\mathbf{A}_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathbf{A}_{n}

and

\xi_{FG}^{*}:=\xi_{{F_{1}.G_{1}(\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{1}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{1}.G_{m}(\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{m}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{m}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{2}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}^{\dagger})}}.

Since all
G_{i}
are
\eta
-regular (and disjoint), by (
vii
),
G^{*}:\mathbf{C}^{*}\to\mathbf{A}_{1}
is
\eta
-regular.
Thus there is a
c
such that
({G^{*},{G^{*}}^{\complement}})({\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}^{\dagger}\otimes c})=\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger}
.
Since
G^{*}
is an iso-reference, the range of
G^{*}
is
\mathbf{A}_{1}
.
Since
{G^{*}}^{\complement}
is disjoint from
G^{*}
(by definition of complements), the ranges of
{G^{*}}^{\complement}
and
G^{*}
commute.
Thus
{G^{*}}^{\complement}(c)
commutes with all of
\mathbf{A}_{1}
, hence
{G^{*}}^{\complement}(c)
is a multiple of the identity, say
\lambda 1
.
Thus

\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger}=({G^{*},{G^{*}}^{\complement}})({\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}^{\dagger}\otimes c})=G^{*}({\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}^{\dagger}})\cdot{G^{*}}^{\complement}(c)=\lambda G^{*}({\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}^{\dagger}}).

Since
\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}
and
\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}
are unit vectors by definition, we have
\lVert{\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger}}\rVert=1

and
\lVert{G^{*}({\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}^{\dagger}})}\rVert\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\lVert{\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}^{\dagger}}\rVert=1

(with
(*)
by
item iv
), we have
\lvert{\lambda}\rvert=1
.
Since
\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger}
and
G^{*}(\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}^{\dagger})
are positive (using the positivity of
G^{*}
,
Lemma 22
),
\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger}=G^{*}(\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}^{\dagger})
implies that
\lambda\geq 0
.
Thus
\lambda=1
.
Thus
G^{*}({\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}^{\dagger}})=\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger}
.
Then
\xi^{*}_{G}=\xi_{\operatorname{im}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger}}=\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}
where the last equality is by definition of
\eta_{\dots}
.

By definition,
\xi_{b}
is in the image of
b=F^{*}(\eta^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})=F^{*}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{*}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{*}}^{\dagger})
.
Since
\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{*}}
and
\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}
are unit vectors,
\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{*}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{*}}^{\dagger},\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger}
are projectors.
Then by
item iii
,
F^{*}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{*}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{*}}^{\dagger}),F_{1}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger})
are projectors.
Thus

F^{*}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{*}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{*}}^{\dagger})\cdot F_{1}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F^{*}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{*}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{*}}^{\dagger})\cdot F^{*}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes 1\otimes\dots\otimes 1)\\
=F^{*}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{*}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{*}}^{\dagger}\cdot(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger}\otimes 1\otimes\dots\otimes 1))\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F^{*}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{*}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{*}}^{\dagger}).

Here
(*)
is by definition of
F^{*}
and
Definition 3
.
And
(**)
uses
\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{*}}=\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}
.

Thus the image of the projector
F^{*}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{*}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}^{*}}^{\dagger})
is a subset of the image of the projector
F_{1}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger})

[
Con13
, Exercise II.3.6]
.
Hence
\xi_{b}
is in the image of
F_{1}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger})
. So
F_{1}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger})\xi_{b}=\xi_{b}
.

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274ptF_{1}.G_{1}(\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{1}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{1}.G_{m}(\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{m}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{m}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{2}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F_{1}\Bigl({{G_{1}}(\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{1}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}{G_{m}}(\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{m}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{m}}^{\dagger})}\Bigr)\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{2}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F_{1}\Bigl({{G^{*}}(\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}^{\dagger})}\Bigr)\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{2}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$\scriptscriptstyle(\!*\!*\!*\!)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F_{1}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}^{\dagger}).

(20)

Here
(*)
follows by
item ix
.
And
(**)
follows by definition of
G^{*}
and
\mathbf{C}^{*}
and the fact that
\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}=\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\mathbf{C}_{m}}=\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{1}}\otimes\dots\otimes\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{m}}
.
And
(*{*}*)
follows because we established that
{G^{*}}(\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}\eta_{\mathbf{C}^{*}}^{\dagger})=\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger}
.
By definition of
\xi_{FG}^{*}
and
\xi_{b}
, (
20
) implies
\xi_{FG}^{*}=\xi_{b}
.

Combining what we have so far, we get:

F_{1}(\xi_{G}^{*}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger})\xi_{b}=F_{1}(\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger})\xi_{b}=\xi_{b}=\xi_{FG}^{*}.

(21)

Then

\displaystyle\hskip-10.00002ptF_{1}\Bigl({G_{1}(\gamma_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}G_{m}(\gamma_{m})}\Bigr)\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{2}(\psi_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{n}(\psi_{n})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\Bigl({F_{1}\Bigl({\bigl({G_{1}(\gamma_{1}\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{1}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}G_{m}(\gamma_{m}\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{n}}^{\dagger})}\bigr)\xi_{G}^{*}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger}}\Bigr)\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{2}(\psi_{2}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\psi_{n}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}^{\dagger})}\Bigr)\cdot\xi_{b}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\Bigl({F_{1}\Bigl({\bigl({G_{1}(\gamma_{1}\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{1}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}G_{m}(\gamma_{m}\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{n}}^{\dagger})}\bigr)}\Bigr)\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{2}(\psi_{2}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\psi_{n}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}^{\dagger})}\Bigr)\cdot F_{1}(\xi_{G}^{*}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger})\xi_{b}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$\scriptscriptstyle(\!*\!*\!*\!)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\Bigl({F_{1}.G_{1}(\gamma_{1}\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{1}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{1}.G_{m}(\gamma_{m}\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{m}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{2}(\psi_{2}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\psi_{n}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}^{\dagger})}\Bigr)\cdot F_{1}(\xi_{G}^{*}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger})\xi_{b}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny\eqref{eq:xi.xiFG}}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\Bigl({F_{1}.G_{1}(\gamma_{1}\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{1}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{1}.G_{m}(\gamma_{m}\eta_{\mathbf{C}_{m}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{2}(\psi_{2}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{2}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\psi_{n}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}^{\dagger})}\Bigr)\cdot\xi_{FG}^{*}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F_{1}.G_{1}(\gamma_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{1}.G_{m}(\gamma_{m})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{2}(\psi_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{n}(\psi_{n}).

Here
(*)
is by definition of
\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
Here
(**)
uses
item iv
with
U:=1
and
V:=\xi_{G}^{*}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger}
.
And
(*{*}*)
uses
item ix
.
This shows (
vi
).

We show (
viii
):

F_{1}(U\psi_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{n}(\psi_{n})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\Bigl({F_{1}(U\psi_{1}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\psi_{n}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}})}\Bigr)\xi_{b}\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F_{1}(U)\Bigl({F_{1}(\psi_{1}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\psi_{n}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}})}\Bigr)\xi_{b}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F_{1}(U)\bigl({F_{1}(\psi_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{n}(\psi_{n})}\bigr).

Here
(*)
is by definition of
\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
And
(**)
by
item iv
.
This shows (
viii
).

We show (
ix
).
By definition
\xi_{b}
is in the image of
b=F^{*}(\eta^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})
.
And since
\xi_{b}
is a unit vector,
\xi_{b}{\xi_{b}}^{\dagger}
is a rank-1 projector.
Since
\eta^{*}
is a unit vector,
\eta^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger}
is a rank-1 projector and so is
F^{*}(\eta^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})
by
item iii
and
item v
(the latter in the degenerate case where
n=1
and
F_{1}=F^{*}
).
The fact that
\xi_{b}
is in the range of
F^{*}(\eta^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})
implies that
\xi_{b}{\xi_{b}}^{\dagger}
and
F^{*}(\eta^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})
project onto the same space, hence
\xi_{b}{\xi_{b}}^{\dagger}=F^{*}(\eta^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})
.
Thus

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\mathord{\lozenge}\Bigl({F_{1}(\psi_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{n}(\psi_{n})}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\mathord{\lozenge}\Bigl({\Bigl({F_{1}(\psi_{1}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{1}}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{1}(\psi_{n}\eta_{\mathbf{A}_{n}}^{\dagger})}\Bigr)\xi_{b}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\mathord{\lozenge}\Bigl({{F^{*}(\psi^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})}\xi_{b}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle={F^{*}(\psi^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})}\xi_{b}{\xi_{b}}^{\dagger}F^{*}(\psi^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle={F^{*}(\psi^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})}F^{*}(\eta^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})F^{*}(\psi^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=F^{*}(\psi^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger}\eta^{*}{\eta^{*}}^{\dagger}\eta^{*}{\psi^{*}}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$\scriptscriptstyle(\!*\!*\!*\!)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F^{*}(\psi^{*}{\psi^{*}}^{\dagger})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F_{1}(\mathord{\lozenge}(\psi_{1}))\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\mathord{\lozenge}(\psi_{n})).

Here
(*)
is by definition of
\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}
.
And
(**)
is by definition of
\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}
.
And
(*{*}*)
follows because
\xi_{b}
is a unit vector.
This shows (
ix
).

We show (
x
).
Let
P_{S}
denote the projector onto
S
.
We have

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274ptF_{1}(\psi_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{n}(\psi_{n})\in F_{1}({S})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{\iff}}F_{1}(\psi_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{n}(\psi_{n})\in\operatorname{im}F_{1}(P_{S})

\displaystyle\iff F_{1}(P_{S})\Bigl({F_{1}(\psi_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{n}(\psi_{n})}\Bigr)=F_{1}(\psi_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{n}(\psi_{n})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny\eqref{lemma:lift.pure:U}}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{\iff}}F_{1}(P_{S}\psi_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{2}(\psi_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{n}(\psi_{n})=F_{1}(\psi_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{2}(\psi_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{n}(\psi_{n})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{\iff}}P_{S}\psi_{1}=\psi_{1}\iff\psi_{1}\in S.

Here
(*)
is by definition of
F_{1}({S})
.
And
(**)
follows since
F_{1}(\cdot)\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{2}(\psi_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F_{n}(\psi_{n})
is linear by (
iii
) and thus injective by (
i
) (using that
\psi_{2},\dots,\psi_{n}\neq 0
).

This shows (
x
).
∎

E.5
Quantum channels, Lemmas
7
and
8

Quantum channels,
Lemma 7

Proof.

We first show (
x
).
By
Lemma 26
,
F(\rho)=U\rho U^{\dagger}
for unitary
U
and all
\rho
.
By
Lemma 34
, this implies that
F|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{A})}
is a quantum channel for any iso-reference
F
.
Since it has inverse
\rho\mapsto U^{\dagger}\rho U
,
F|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{A})}
is bijective.
Since
F,G
and thus also
F\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G
are iso-references, we have that
F|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{A})},G|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{B})},(F\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G)|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{A}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{B})}
are quantum channels.
Thus
F|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{A})}\otimes G|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{B})}
is also a quantum channel (
item iv
).
For all trace-class
\rho,\sigma
,

(F\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G)|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{A}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{B})}(\rho\otimes\sigma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}(F\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G)(\rho\otimes\sigma)=F(\rho)\otimes G(\sigma)\\
=F|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{A})}(\rho)\otimes G|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{B})}(\sigma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}(F|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{A})}\otimes G|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{B})})(\rho\otimes\sigma)

Here
(*)
uses that
\rho\otimes\sigma
is trace-class by
Lemma 32
.
And
(**)
uses
item i
.
Since they coincide on all
\rho\otimes\sigma
, we have
(F\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}G)|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{A}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{B})}=F|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{A})}\otimes G|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{B})}
by
Lemma 37
.
This shows (
x
).

We show (
i
).

({F,F^{\complement}})
is an iso-reference by definition of
F^{\complement}
.
Thus
({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}
is also an iso-reference.
Then by (
x
),
({F,F^{\complement}})
and
({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}
are quantum channels.
Thus if
\mathcal{E}
is a quantum (sub)channel, then the composition
F({\mathcal{E}})=({F,F^{\complement}})\circ(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}
is as well.
This shows (
i
).

We show (
ii
).
If
F,G
are complements, then
G
and
F^{\complement}
are equivalent (Law
4
).
That means there is an iso-reference
I
such that
G=F^{\complement}\circ I
.
Thus

\displaystyle FG\circ(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ FG^{-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({F,F^{\complement}})\circ(\mathit{id}\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}I)\circ(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ(\mathit{id}\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}I^{-1})\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny\eqref{lemma:isoreg:channel}}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({F,F^{\complement}})\circ(\mathit{id}\otimes I)\circ(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ(\mathit{id}\otimes I^{-1})\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({F,F^{\complement}})\circ\bigl({\mathcal{E}\otimes(I\circ I^{-1})}\bigr)\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=({F,F^{\complement}})\circ({\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id}})\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}=F({\mathcal{E}}).

Here
(*)
uses Law
2
and
(**)
uses
item iii
.
This shows (
ii
).

We show (
iii
).

F({\mathcal{E}})\circ F({\mathcal{F}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({F,F^{\complement}})\circ(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}\circ({F,F^{\complement}})\circ(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({F,F^{\complement}})\circ(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$\scriptscriptstyle(\!*\!*\!*\!)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({F,F^{\complement}})\circ\bigl({(\mathcal{E}\circ\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathit{id}}\bigr)\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F({\mathcal{E}\circ\mathcal{F}}).

Here
(*)
is by definition of
F({\mathcal{E}}),F({\mathcal{F}})
.
And
(**)
uses that
({F,F^{\complement}})
is an iso-reference and thus bijective.
And
(*{*}*)
uses
item iii
.
This shows (
iii
).

We show (
iv
).
By Law
4
, we have that
F
and
({G,FG^{\complement}})
are complements.
And that
G
and
({F,FG^{\complement}})
are complements.

\displaystyle F(\mathcal{E})\circ G(\mathcal{F})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny\eqref{lemma:lift.channel:choice.compl}}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({F,({G,FG^{\complement}})})\circ(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ({F,({G,FG^{\complement}})})^{-1}\circ({G,({F,FG^{\complement}})})\circ(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ({G,({F,FG^{\complement}})})^{-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({FG,FG^{\complement}})\circ\alpha^{\prime}\circ(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ{\alpha}\circ({FG,FG^{\complement}})^{-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\circ({FG,FG^{\complement}})\circ(\sigma\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}\mathit{id})\circ\alpha^{\prime}\circ(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ{\alpha}\circ(\sigma\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}\mathit{id})\circ({FG,FG^{\complement}})^{-1}

\displaystyle=\bigl({FG,FG^{\complement}}\bigr)\circ{\alpha^{\prime}\circ(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ\alpha\circ(\sigma\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}\mathit{id})\circ\alpha^{\prime}\circ(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ\alpha\circ(\sigma\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}\mathit{id})}{}\circ\bigl({FG,FG^{\complement}}\bigr)^{-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny\eqref{lemma:isoreg:channel}}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\bigl({FG,FG^{\complement}}\bigr)\circ\underbrace{\alpha^{\prime}\circ(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ\alpha\circ(\sigma\otimes\mathit{id})\circ\alpha^{\prime}\circ(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ\alpha\circ(\sigma\otimes\mathit{id})}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\ (\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathit{id}}{}\circ\bigl({FG,FG^{\complement}}\bigr)^{-1}

\displaystyle=FG(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{F}).

Here
(*)
uses Laws
2
,
2
,
2
and
{\alpha^{\prime}}^{-1}=\alpha
.
And
(**)
is verified by evaluating the left and right hand side of the equality on all
(\rho\otimes\tau)\otimes\pi
for trace-class
\rho,\tau,\pi
.
Both sides evaluate to
(\mathcal{E}(\rho)\otimes\mathcal{F}(\tau))\otimes\pi
, i.e., they are equal on all
(\rho\otimes\tau)\otimes\pi
.
Furthermore, both sides are quantum channels by (
x
).
Thus by
Lemma 37
, both sides are equal.
So
(**)
holds.
This shows (
iv
).

We show (
v
). We have

F({\mathit{id}})=({F,F^{\complement}})\circ(\mathit{id}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({F,F^{\complement}})\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\mathit{id}.

Here
(*)
follows because
\mathit{id}\otimes\mathit{id}=\mathit{id}
(
item vi
).
And
(**)
since
({F,F^{\complement}})
is an iso-reference.
This shows (
v
).

We show (
vi
).
We abbreviate
F^{*}:=({F_{2},\dots,F_{n}})
and
\rho^{*}:=\rho_{2}\otimes\dots\otimes\rho_{n}
. (If
n=1
, we let
F^{*}
be the unit reference from
Lemma 2
and
\rho^{*}:=1\in\mathbb{C}
.)
Note that
F_{1}
and
F^{*}
are complements because
F_{1},\dots,F_{n}
is a partition.
We have

\displaystyle\hskip-56.9055ptF_{1}({\mathcal{E}})\bigl({F_{1}(\rho_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\rho_{n})}\bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F_{1}({\mathcal{E}})\circ({F_{1},F^{*}})(\rho_{1}\otimes\rho^{*})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny\eqref{lemma:lift.channel:choice.compl}}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({F_{1},F^{*}})\circ(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ({F_{1},F^{*}})^{-1}\circ({F_{1},F^{*}})(\rho_{1}\otimes\rho^{*})=({F_{1},F^{*}})\circ(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ(\rho_{1}\otimes\rho^{*})

\displaystyle=({F_{1},F^{*}})\bigl({\mathcal{E}(\rho_{1})\otimes\rho^{*}}\bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F_{1}\bigl({\mathcal{E}(\rho_{1})}\bigr)\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{2}(\rho_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\rho_{n})

Here
(*)
is by definition of
\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}
.
This shows (
vi
).

We show (
vii
).

\displaystyle F(G(\mathcal{E}))

\displaystyle=({F,F^{\complement}})\circ\Bigl({\Bigl({({G,G^{\complement}})\circ(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ({G,G^{\complement}})^{-1}}\Bigr)\otimes\mathit{id}}\Bigr)\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({F,F^{\complement}})\circ\bigl({({G,G^{\complement}})\otimes\mathit{id}}\bigr)\circ\bigl({(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})\otimes\mathit{id}}\bigr)\circ\bigl({({G,G^{\complement}})^{-1}\otimes\mathit{id}}\bigr)\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny\eqref{lemma:isoreg:channel}}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({F,F^{\complement}})\circ\bigl({({G,G^{\complement}})\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}\mathit{id}}\bigr)\circ\bigl({(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})\otimes\mathit{id}}\bigr)\circ\bigl({({G,G^{\complement}})\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}\mathit{id}}\bigr)^{-1}\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({({F.G,F.G^{\complement}}),F^{\complement}})\circ\bigl({(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})\otimes\mathit{id}}\bigr)\circ({({F.G,F.G^{\complement}}),F^{\complement}})^{-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$\scriptscriptstyle(\!*\!*\!*\!)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({F.G,({F.G^{\complement},F^{\complement}})})\circ\underbrace{\alpha\circ\bigl({(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})\otimes\mathit{id}}\bigr)\circ\alpha^{\prime}}_{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$\scriptscriptstyle(\!*\!*\!*\!*\!)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\ \mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id}}{}\circ({F.G,({F.G^{\complement},F^{\complement}})})^{-1}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$\bigl(\overset{**}{*{*}*}\bigr)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}(F.G)(\mathcal{E}).

Here
(*)
uses
item iii
, (
vi
).
And
(**)
uses Laws
2
and
2
.
And
(*{*}*)
uses Law
2
.
And
(*{*}{*}*)
is verified by evaluating the left and right hand side of the equality on all
\rho\otimes(\tau\otimes\pi)
for trace-class
\rho,\tau,\pi
.
Both sides evaluate to
\mathcal{E}(\rho)\otimes(\tau\otimes\pi)
.
Thus both sides are equal as quantum channels by
Lemma 37
.
So
(*{*}{*}*)
holds.
And
(\overset{\scriptstyle**}{\scriptstyle*{*}*})
follows with (
ii
) using that
F.G
and
({F.G^{\complement},F^{\complement}})
are complements by Law (
4
).
This shows (
vii
).

We show (
viii
).
Let
u
be the unit reference with domain
\mathbb{C}
from
Lemma 2
.
Hence by Law
4
,
\sigma,u
are complements.
We have
({\sigma,u})(x\otimes 1)=\sigma(x)
.
Since
({\sigma,u})
and
\sigma
are both iso-references and thus invertible, this implies
({\sigma,u})^{-1}(y)=\sigma^{-1}(y)\otimes 1_{\mathbb{C}}
.
Furthermore,
\sigma(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{F})=({\sigma,u})\circ\bigl({(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathit{id}}\bigr)\circ({\sigma,u})^{-1}
by (
ii
).
Hence for all trace-class
\rho
,
\tau
, we have

\rho\otimes\tau\xmapsto{({\sigma,u})^{-1}}(\tau\otimes\rho)\otimes 1_{\mathbb{C}}\xmapsto{{(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathit{id}}}\bigl({\mathcal{E}(\tau)\otimes\mathcal{F}(\rho)}\bigr)\otimes 1_{\mathbb{C}}\xmapsto{({\sigma,u})}\mathcal{F}(\rho)\otimes\mathcal{E}(\tau)=(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{E})(\rho\otimes\tau).

(Note that in this calculation, we used that
1_{\mathbb{C}}
is trace-class.
We used this when evaluating
{(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathit{id}}
which, as a tensor product of quantum channels, is only defined on trace-class operators.)
Thus
\sigma(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{F})(\rho\otimes\tau)=(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{E})(\rho\otimes\tau)
for all
\rho,\tau
.
By
Lemma 37
, this implies
\sigma(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{F})=\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{E}
.
This shows (
viii
).

We show (
ix
).
Let
u
be the unit reference with domain
\mathbb{C}
from
Lemma 2
.
Hence by Law
4
,
\alpha,u
are complements.
We have
({\alpha,u})(x\otimes 1)=\alpha(x)
.
Since
({\alpha,u})
and
\alpha
are both iso-references and thus invertible, this implies
({\alpha,u})^{-1}(y)=\alpha^{-1}(y)\otimes 1_{\mathbb{C}}
.
Furthermore,
\alpha(\mathcal{E}\otimes(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{G}))=({\alpha,u})\circ\bigl({(\mathcal{E}\otimes(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{G}))\otimes\mathit{id}}\bigr)\circ({\alpha,u})^{-1}
by (
ii
).
Hence for all trace-class
\rho
,
\tau
,
\gamma
we have

(\rho\otimes\tau)\otimes\gamma\xmapsto{({\alpha,u})^{-1}}\bigl({\rho\otimes(\tau\otimes\gamma)}\bigr)\otimes 1_{\mathbb{C}}\xmapsto{{\bigl({\mathcal{E}\otimes(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{G})}\bigr)\otimes\mathit{id}}}\bigl({\mathcal{E}(\rho)\otimes(\mathcal{F}(\tau)\otimes\mathcal{G}(\gamma))}\bigr)\otimes 1_{\mathbb{C}}\\
\xmapsto{({\alpha,u})}{(\mathcal{E}(\rho)\otimes\mathcal{F}(\tau))\otimes\mathcal{G}(\gamma)}=\bigl({(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathcal{G}}\bigr)\bigl({(\rho\otimes\tau)\otimes\gamma}\bigr)

(Like in the proof of (
viii
), we used that
1_{\mathbb{C}}
is trace-class.)
Thus
\alpha(\mathcal{E}\otimes(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{G}))\bigl({(\rho\otimes\tau)\otimes\gamma}\bigr)=\bigl({(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathcal{G}}\bigr)\bigl({(\rho\otimes\tau)\otimes\gamma}\bigr)
for all
\rho,\tau,\gamma
.
By
Lemma 37
, this implies
\alpha\bigl({\mathcal{E}\otimes(\mathcal{F}\otimes\mathcal{G})}\bigr)={(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{F})\otimes\mathcal{G}}
.
The
\alpha^{\prime}
case of (
ix
) is shown analogously.
This shows (
ix
).

We show (
xi
).
We first show

\mathbf{Sem}\bigl({F(U)}\bigr)\circ({F,F^{\complement}})=F\bigl({\mathbf{Sem}(U)}\bigr)\circ({F,F^{\complement}}).

(22)

By
Lemma 37
, it is sufficient to show this equality on all
\tau\otimes\sigma
.

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\mathbf{Sem}\bigl({F(U)}\bigr)\circ({F,F^{\complement}})(\tau\otimes\sigma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\mathbf{Sem}\bigl({F(U)}\bigr)(F(\tau)F^{\complement}(\sigma))=F(U)F(\tau)F^{\complement}(\sigma)F(U)^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=F(U)F(\tau)F^{\complement}(\sigma)F(U^{\dagger})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F(U)F(\tau)F(U^{\dagger})F^{\complement}(\sigma)=F(U\tau U^{\dagger})F^{\complement}(\sigma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({F,F^{\complement}})\bigl({U\tau U^{\dagger}\otimes\sigma}\bigr)

\displaystyle=({F,F^{\complement}})\circ\bigl({\mathbf{Sem}(U)\otimes\mathit{id}}\bigr)(\tau\otimes\sigma)=({F,F^{\complement}})\circ\bigl({\mathbf{Sem}(U)\otimes\mathit{id}}\bigr)\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}\circ({F,F^{\complement}})(\tau\otimes\sigma)

\displaystyle=F(\mathbf{Sem}(U))\circ({F,F^{\complement}})(\tau\otimes\sigma).

Here
(*)
is by the definition of pairs of references (
Definition 3
).
And
(**)
follows because
F,F^{\complement}
are disjoint.

This this holds for all
\tau,\sigma
, we conclude (
22
).
Since
({F,F^{\complement}})
is an iso-register and thus surjective,

\mathbf{Sem}(F(U))=F(\mathbf{Sem}(U))
and thus (
xi
) follows.
∎

Kraus-channels,
Lemma 8

Proof.

We show (
i
).
If
\mathcal{E}
is a Kraus-(sub)channel, then
\mathcal{E}(\rho)
is of the form
\mathcal{E}(\rho)=\sum_{i}M_{i}\rho M_{i}^{\dagger}
with
\sum_{i}M_{i}^{\dagger}M_{i}\leqq 1
.
(Where
\leqq
means
=
or
\leq
depending on whether we are talking about Kraus-channels or -subchannels.)
Then
F({\mathcal{E}})(\rho)=\sum_{i}F(M_{i})\rho F(M_{i})^{\dagger}
.
And

\sum_{i}F(M_{i})^{\dagger}F(M_{i})=\sum_{i}F(M_{i}M_{i}^{\dagger})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F\Bigl({\sum_{i}M_{i}M_{i}^{\dagger}}\Bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{\leqq}}F(1)=1

Here
(*)
follows by
Lemma 31
.
And
(**)
follows because
\sum_{i}M_{i}M_{i}^{\dagger}\leqq 1
and
F
is positive (
Lemma 22
) and thus monotonous.
This shows that
F({\mathcal{E}})
is a Kraus-(sub)channel.

We show (
iii
).
Let
\mathcal{E}(\rho)=\sum_{i}M_{i}\rho M_{i}^{\dagger}
be a Kraus-(sub)channel.
We write
F({\mathcal{E}})_{1}
for
F(\mathcal{E})
as defined for quantum (sub)channels,
and
F({\mathcal{E}})_{2}
for
F(\mathcal{E})
as defined for Kraus-(sub)channels.
Fix trace-class
\sigma,\tau
.

\displaystyle F({\mathcal{E}})_{1}\circ({F,F^{\complement}})(\sigma\otimes\tau)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({F,F^{\complement}})\circ(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})(\sigma\otimes\tau)=({F,F^{\complement}})(\mathcal{E}(\sigma)\otimes\tau)=F(\mathcal{E}(\sigma))\cdot F^{\complement}(\tau)

\displaystyle=F\Bigl({\sum\nolimits_{i}M_{i}\sigma M_{i}^{\dagger}}\Bigr)\cdot F^{\complement}(\tau)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\sum\nolimits_{i}F({M_{i}\sigma M_{i}^{\dagger}})\cdot F^{\complement}(\tau)=\sum\nolimits_{i}F(M_{1})F(\sigma)F(M_{i})^{\dagger}\cdot F^{\complement}(\tau)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$\scriptscriptstyle(\!*\!*\!*\!)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\sum\nolimits_{i}F(M_{1})F(\sigma)F(M_{i})^{\dagger}F^{\complement}(\tau)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$\scriptscriptstyle(\!*\!*\!*\!*\!)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\sum\nolimits_{i}F(M_{1})F(\sigma)F^{\complement}(\tau)F(M_{i})^{\dagger}=\sum\nolimits_{i}F(M_{1})({F,F^{\complement}})(\sigma\otimes\tau)F(M_{i})^{\dagger}

\displaystyle=F({\mathcal{E}})_{2}\circ({F,F^{\complement}})(\sigma\otimes\tau).

Here
(*)
is by definition of
F({\mathcal{E}})_{1}
.
And
(**)
follows by
Lemma 31
.
And
(*{*}*)
follows because multiplication from the right is easily seen to be SOT-continuous (and the sum converges in the SOT), so we can pull
F^{\complement}(\tau)
into the sum.
And
(*{*}{*}*)
follows since
F
,
F^{\complement}
are disjoint and thus
F(M_{i}^{\dagger})
and
F^{\complement}(\tau)
commute.

Thus the quantum subchannels
F({\mathcal{E}})_{1}\circ({F,F^{\complement}})

and
F({\mathcal{E}})_{2}\circ({F,F^{\complement}})
are equal on all
\sigma\otimes\tau
.
By
Lemma 37
, this implies
F({\mathcal{E}})_{1}\circ({F,F^{\complement}})=F({\mathcal{E}})_{2}\circ({F,F^{\complement}})
.
Since
({F,F^{\complement}})
is an iso-reference and thus invertible, this implies
F({\mathcal{E}})_{1}=F({\mathcal{E}})_{2}
.
This shows (
iii
).

Finally, (
ii
) follows from (
iii
) since the definition of
F({\mathcal{E}})
for quantum subchannels does not depend on the choice of
M_{i}
.
∎

E.6
Partial trace,
Lemma 9

Proof.

We show (
i
).
By
[
Con00
, Theorem 18.11 (c)]
,
\operatorname{tr}:T(\mathcal{H}_{A^{\prime}})\to\mathbb{C}
is a positive linear functional.
By
[
Con00
, Lemma 34.5]
, positive linear functionals are completely positive.
Thus
\operatorname{tr}
is completely positive.
Note that
B(\mathbb{C})=T(\mathbb{C})
and we can identify both with
\mathbb{C}
via the trivial isomorphism
c\in\mathbb{C}\mapsto c\cdot 1
.
That is,
\operatorname{tr}:T(\mathcal{H}_{A^{\prime}})\to T(\mathbb{C})
.
Then
\operatorname{tr}
is trace-preserving.
Hence
\operatorname{tr}
is a quantum channel.
Then
\mathit{id}\otimes\operatorname{tr}:T(\mathcal{H}_{A}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{A^{\prime}})\to T(\mathcal{H}_{A}\otimes\mathbb{C})
exists and is a quantum channel by
item iv
.
Let
u:B(\mathbb{C})\to\mathbf{B}
,
u(c):=c\cdot 1_{\mathbf{B}}
.
Then
u
is a unit reference by
Lemma 2
.
Thus
({\mathit{id},u})
is an iso-reference and thus a quantum channel (
item x
).
Hence
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}:=({\mathit{id},u})\circ(\mathit{id}\otimes\operatorname{tr})
is a quantum channel.
Furthermore

\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}(\rho\otimes\sigma)=({\mathit{id},u})\circ(\mathit{id}\otimes\operatorname{tr})(\rho\otimes\sigma)=({\mathit{id},u})(\rho\otimes\operatorname{tr}\sigma)=\rho\cdot(\operatorname{tr}\sigma\cdot 1_{\mathbf{B}})=\rho\cdot\operatorname{tr}\sigma.

Hence
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}
defined this way satisfies the definition of
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}
given in
Section 9.6
.
So
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}
exists (and is a quantum channel).
Uniqueness of
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}
follows by
Lemma 37
.
This shows that
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}
is well-defined and thus also
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}
.
This shows (
i
).

We show (
ii
).
In the proof of (
i
), we showed that
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}
is a quantum channel.

({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}
is an iso-reference and thus a quantum channel (
item x
).
Thus
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}={\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}
is a quantum channel.
This shows (
ii
).

We show (
iii
).
Since
F,G
are complements, by Law
4
,
F^{\complement}
and
G
are equivalent.
Thus
G=F^{\complement}\circ I
for some iso-reference
I
.
By
item x
,
I
is a quantum channel.
Then
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}\circ(\mathit{id}\otimes I)
is a quantum channel as well and for all trace-class
\rho,\sigma
,

{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}\circ(\mathit{id}\otimes I)(\rho\otimes\sigma)=\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}(\rho\otimes I(\sigma))=\rho\cdot\operatorname{tr}I(\sigma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\rho\cdot\operatorname{tr}\sigma=\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}(\rho\otimes\sigma).

Here
(*)
uses that
I
is a quantum channel and thus trace-preserving by definition.
By
Lemma 37
, this implies
{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}\circ(\mathit{id}\otimes I)=\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}
as quantum channels.
Then

\displaystyle\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}

\displaystyle={\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}={\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}\circ({F,G\circ I^{-1}})^{-1}={\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}\circ\bigl({({F,G})\circ(\mathit{id}\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}I^{-1})}\bigr)^{-1}

\displaystyle={\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}\circ(\mathit{id}\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}I)\circ{({F,G})}^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}\circ(\mathit{id}\otimes I)\circ{({F,G})}^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}\circ{({F,G})}^{-1}.

(As an equality of functions on the space of trace-class operators.)
Here
(*)
uses
item x
.
And
(**)
uses that
{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}\circ(\mathit{id}\otimes I)=\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}
as quantum channels (shown above) and the fact that
{({F,G})}^{-1}
is a quantum channel (
item x
).
This shows (
iii
).

We show (
iv
).
Let
u:B(\mathbb{C})\to\mathbf{B}
,
u(c):=c\cdot 1_{\mathbf{B}}
.
Then
u
is a unit reference (
Lemma 2
).
Since
F
is an iso-reference,
F,u
are complements (Law
4
).
For trace-class
\rho
,

{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}}\circ F|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{A})}(\rho)={\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}}\circ({F,u})(\rho\otimes 1_{\mathbb{C}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny\eqref{lemma:partial.tr:choice.compl}}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}\circ({F,u})^{-1}\circ({F,u})(\rho\otimes 1_{\mathbb{C}})={\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}(\rho\otimes 1_{\mathbb{C}})=\rho.

(Note that
1_{\mathbb{C}}
is trace-class, and iso-references are quantum channels by
item x
, so we do not apply
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F},\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}
to anything outside their domain.)
Thus
{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}}\circ F|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{A})}=\mathit{id}
.
Since
F
is an iso-reference,
F|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{A})}
is a bijection
T(\mathcal{H}_{A})\to T(\mathcal{H}_{A})
by
item x
.
Thus
{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}}\circ F|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{A})}=\mathit{id}
implies
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}=F^{-1}|_{T(\mathcal{H}_{A})}
.
This shows (
iv
).

We show (
v
).
We have

\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}({F,G})(\rho\otimes\sigma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny\eqref{lemma:partial.tr:choice.compl}}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}\circ({F,G})^{-1}\circ({F,G})(\rho\otimes\sigma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}(\rho\otimes\sigma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\rho\cdot\operatorname{tr}\sigma.

Here
(*)
uses that
({F,G})
is an iso-reference and thus bijective.
And
(**)
is by definition of
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}
.
This shows (
v
).

We show (
vi
).
By
item iii
, the order of the
{F_{1}(\rho_{1}),\dots,F_{n}(\rho_{n})}
does not matter.
Thus without loss of generality, we can assume
i=1
.
Let
F^{*}:=({F_{2},\dots,F_{n}})
and
\rho^{*}:=\rho_{2}\otimes\dots\otimes\rho_{n}
.
(If
n=1
, we let
F^{*}
be the unit reference from
Lemma 2
and
\rho^{*}:=1_{\mathbb{C}}
.)
Since
F_{1},\dots,F_{n}
is a partition,
F_{1},F^{*}
are complements.
Then

\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F_{1}}\bigl({F_{1}(\rho_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F_{n}(\rho_{n})}\bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F_{1}}\bigl({({F_{1},\dots,F_{n}})(\rho_{1}\otimes\dots\otimes\rho_{n})}\bigr)\\
=\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F_{1}}\bigl({({F_{1},F^{*}})(\rho_{1}\otimes\rho^{*})}\bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny\eqref{lemma:partial.tr:pair}}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\rho_{1}\otimes\operatorname{tr}\rho^{*}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\rho_{i}\cdot\prod\nolimits_{j=2}^{n}\operatorname{tr}\rho_{i}.

Here
(*)
is by definition of
\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}
.
And
(**)
is by
Lemma 32
.
(Recall that
\otimes
and
({\cdot,\cdot})
are right-associative.)

This shows (
vi
).

We show (
vii
).
For any trace-class
\rho,\sigma,\tau
, we have

\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F.G}\bigl({F.G,({F^{\complement},F.G^{\complement}})}\bigr)\bigl({\rho\otimes(\tau\otimes\sigma)}\bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}\circ\bigl({F.G,({F^{\complement},F.G^{\complement}})}\bigr)^{-1}\circ\bigl({F.G,({F^{\complement},F.G^{\complement}})}\bigr)\bigl({\rho\otimes(\tau\otimes\sigma)}\bigr)\\
=\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}\bigl({\rho\otimes(\tau\otimes\sigma)}\bigr)=\rho\cdot\operatorname{tr}(\tau\otimes\sigma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\rho\cdot\operatorname{tr}\sigma\cdot\operatorname{tr}\tau.

(23)

Here
(*)
is by (
iii
) using the fact that
{({F^{\complement},F.G^{\complement}})}
is a complement of
F.G
by Law
4
.
And
(**)
is by
Lemma 32
.
And also

\displaystyle\hskip-28.45274pt\bigl({F.G,({F^{\complement},F.G^{\complement}})}\bigr)\bigl({\rho\otimes(\tau\otimes\sigma)}\bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}({F,F^{\complement}})\circ\bigl({({G,G^{\complement}})\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}\mathit{id}}\bigr)\bigl({(\rho\otimes\sigma)\otimes\tau}\bigr)

\displaystyle\xmapsto{({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}}\bigl({({G,G^{\complement}})\otimes_{\mathbf{r}}\mathit{id}}\bigr)\bigl({(\rho\otimes\sigma)\otimes\tau}\bigr)=({G,G^{\complement}})(\rho\otimes\sigma)\otimes\tau\xmapsto{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}({G,G^{\complement}})(\rho\otimes\sigma)\cdot\operatorname{tr}\tau

\displaystyle\xmapsto{({G,G^{\complement}})^{-1}}\rho\otimes\sigma\cdot\operatorname{tr}\tau\xmapsto{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}\rho\cdot\operatorname{tr}\sigma\cdot\operatorname{tr}\tau.

Here
(*)
is easily seen by evaluating both sides.
Since
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}={\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}
and
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow G}={\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}\circ({G,G^{\complement}})^{-1}
, the
\xmapsto{\dots}
correspond to applying
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow G}\circ\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}
.
Thus, with (
23
),

\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow G}\circ\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}\circ\bigl({F.G,({F^{\complement},F.G^{\complement}})}\bigr)\bigl({\rho\otimes(\tau\otimes\sigma)}\bigr)=\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F.G}\circ\bigl({F.G,({F^{\complement},F.G^{\complement}})}\bigr)\bigl({\rho\otimes(\tau\otimes\sigma)}\bigr).

Since
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F},\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow G},\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F.G}
are quantum channels by (
ii
),
and
\bigl({F.G,({F^{\complement},F.G^{\complement}})}\bigr)
is an iso-reference and thus a quantum channel by
item x
,
the function compositions on the left and right hand side are quantum channels that are equal on all
\rho\otimes(\tau\otimes\sigma)
.
Thus by
Lemma 37
,
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow G}\circ\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}\circ\bigl({F.G,({F^{\complement},F.G^{\complement}})}\bigr)=\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F.G}\circ\bigl({F.G,({F^{\complement},F.G^{\complement}})}\bigr)
.
Since
\bigl({F.G,({F^{\complement},F.G^{\complement}})}\bigr)
is an iso-reference, this implies
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow G}\circ\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}=\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F.G}
.
This shows (
vii
).

We show (
viii
).
For trace-class
\rho,\sigma
, we have

{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}}\circ F({\mathcal{E}})\circ({F,F^{\complement}})(\rho\otimes\sigma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}\circ({F,F^{\complement}})\circ(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})\circ({F,F^{\complement}})^{-1}\circ({F,F^{\complement}})(\rho\otimes\sigma)\\
={\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}}\circ(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})(\rho\otimes\sigma)=\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow\mathsf{Fst}}(\mathcal{E}(\rho)\otimes\sigma)=\mathcal{E}(\rho)\cdot\operatorname{tr}\sigma=\mathcal{E}(\rho\cdot\operatorname{tr}\sigma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny\eqref{lemma:partial.tr:pair}}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\mathcal{E}\circ{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}}\circ({F,F^{\complement}})(\rho\otimes\sigma).

Here
(*)
is by definition of
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}
and of
F({\mathcal{E}})
.
Since
\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}
,
\mathcal{E}
,
F({\mathcal{E}})
, and
({F,F^{\complement}})
are quantum subchannels, this implies that
{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}}\circ F({\mathcal{E}})\circ({F,F^{\complement}})=\mathcal{E}\circ\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}\circ({F,F^{\complement}})
by
Lemma 37
.
Since
({F,F^{\complement}})
is an iso-reference, this implies
{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}}\circ F({\mathcal{E}})=\mathcal{E}\circ\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}
.
This shows (
viii
).

We show (
ix
).
By Law
4
,
F
and
({G,FG^{\complement}})
are complements.
Let
Z:=({F,({G,FG^{\complement}})})
.

Z
is an iso-reference because
F
and
({G,FG^{\complement}})
are complements.
By
item iv
, (
v
),
Z({\mathit{id}\otimes(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})})=G({\mathcal{E}})
.
Thus for all trace-class
\rho,\sigma
, we have

{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}}\circ G({\mathcal{E}})\circ Z(\rho\otimes\sigma)={\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}}\circ Z({\mathit{id}\otimes(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})})\circ Z(\rho\otimes\sigma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}}\circ Z\bigl({\bigl({\mathit{id}\otimes(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})}\bigr)\bigl({\rho\otimes\sigma}\bigr)}\bigr)\\
={\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}}\circ Z\bigl({\rho\otimes(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})(\sigma)}\bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny\eqref{lemma:partial.tr:pair}}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\rho\cdot\operatorname{tr}(\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id})(\sigma)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\rho\cdot\operatorname{tr}\sigma\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny\eqref{lemma:partial.tr:pair}}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}}\circ Z(\rho\otimes\sigma).

Here
(*)
is by
item vi
(in the special case where
Z
is a one-element partition).
And
(**)
follows since
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathit{id}
is a quantum channel by
item iv
and thus trace-preserving.
Since
Z
is an iso-reference, it is a quantum channel (
item x
).
Thus the function compositions on the left and right hand side are quantum channels.
And equal on all
\rho\otimes\sigma
.
By
Lemma 37
, this implies
{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}}\circ G({\mathcal{E}})\circ Z={\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}}\circ Z
as quantum channels.
Since
Z
is and iso-reference and thus bijective, this implies
{\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}}\circ G({\mathcal{E}})=\operatorname{tr}_{\downarrow F}
.
This shows (
ix
).
∎

E.7
Measurements, Lemmas
10
,
11
, and
12

Projective measurements,
Lemma 10

Proof.

We show (
i
).
Since
M=\{{P_{i}}\}_{i}
is a projective measurement,
P_{i}
are projectors,
P_{i}P_{j}=0
(
i\neq j
), and
\sum_{i}P_{i}=1
.
By definition,
F({M})=\{{F(P_{i})}\}_{i}
.
By
item iii
,
F(P_{i})
is a projector.
For
i\neq j
, we have
F(P_{i})F(P_{j})=F(P_{i}P_{j})=F(0)=0
.
And

\sum_{i}F(P_{i})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F\Bigl({\sum_{i}P_{i}}\Bigr)=F(1)=1.

Here
(*)
is by
Lemma 31
.
This shows (
i
).

We show (
ii
).
We have

\displaystyle F(U)\,F({M})

\displaystyle=F(U)\bigl\{{F(P_{i})}\bigr\}_{i}=\bigl\{{F(U)F(P_{i})F(U)^{\dagger}}\bigr\}_{i}=\bigl\{{F(UP_{i}U^{\dagger})}\bigr\}_{i}=F\bigl({\{{UP_{i}U^{\dagger}}\}_{i}}\bigr)=F({UM})

and

F({UM})=\bigl\{{F(UP_{i}U^{\dagger})}\bigr\}_{i}=\bigl\{{F(\mathsf{Sd}_{U}(P_{i}))}\bigr\}_{i}=\bigl\{{F.\mathsf{Sd}_{U}(P_{i})}\bigr\}_{i}=({F.\mathsf{Sd}_{U}})({M}).

This shows (
ii
).

We show (
iii
).
By definition
F({M})=\{F(P_{i})\}_{i}
.
By definition of non-normalized post-measurement states, we have
\psi_{1}^{\prime}=P_{j}\psi_{1}
and
\psi^{\prime}=F(P_{1})\psi
.
Then

\psi^{\prime}=F(P_{1})\psi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F(P_{1}\psi_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F(\psi_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F(\psi_{n})=F(\psi_{1}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F(\psi_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F(\psi_{n}).

Here
(*)
is by
item viii
and the definition of
\psi
.
This shows (
iii
).

We show (
iv
).
By definition
F({M})=\{F(P_{i})\}_{i}
.
By definition of non-normalized post-measurement states, we have
\rho_{1}^{\prime}=P_{j}\rho_{1}P_{j}^{\dagger}
and
\rho^{\prime}=F(P_{1})\rho F(P_{j})^{\dagger}
.
Then

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime}

\displaystyle=F(P_{1})\rho F(P_{1})^{\dagger}=F(P_{1})\rho F(P_{1}^{\dagger})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F(P_{1}\rho_{1}P_{1}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F(\rho_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F(\rho_{n})

\displaystyle=F(\rho_{1}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F(\rho_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F(\rho_{n}).

Here
(*)
is by
item iv
and the definition of
\rho
.
This shows (
iv
).
∎

POVMs,
Lemma 11

Proof.

We show (
i
).
Since
M
is a POVM,
M_{i}
are positive operators, and
\sum_{i}M_{i}=1
.
By definition,
F({M})=\{{F(M_{i})}\}_{i}
.
Since
F
is positive (
Lemma 22
),
F(P_{i})
is a positive operator.
And

\sum_{i}F(M_{i})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F\Bigl({\sum_{i}M_{i}}\Bigr)=F(1)=1.

Here
(*)
is by
Lemma 31
.
This shows (
i
).

We show (
ii
).
We have

\displaystyle F(U)\,F({M})

\displaystyle=F(U)\bigl\{{F(M_{i})}\bigr\}_{i}=\bigl\{{F(U)F(P_{i})F(U)^{\dagger}}\bigr\}_{i}=\bigl\{{F(UM_{i}U^{\dagger})}\bigr\}_{i}=F\bigl({\{{UM_{i}U^{\dagger}}\}_{i}}\bigr)=F({UM})

and

F({UM})=\bigl\{{F(UM_{i}U^{\dagger})}\bigr\}_{i}=\bigl\{{F(\mathsf{Sd}_{U}(M_{i}))}\bigr\}_{i}=\bigl\{{F.\mathsf{Sd}_{U}(M_{i})}\bigr\}_{i}=({F.\mathsf{Sd}_{U}})({M}).

This shows (
ii
).

We show (
iii
).
By
item ii
, without loss of generality, we can assume
k=1
.
By definition, the probability of outcome
j
using
M
given
\psi_{1}
is
\lVert{\sqrt{M_{j}}\psi_{1}}\rVert^{2}
.
And the probability of outcome
j
using
F({M})
given
\psi
is
\lVert{\sqrt{F(M_{j})}\psi}\rVert^{2}
.
So we have to show
\bigl\lVert{\sqrt{M_{j}}\psi_{1}}\bigr\rVert=\bigl\lVert{\sqrt{F(M_{j})}\psi}\bigr\rVert
.
Note that
F(\sqrt{M_{j}})
is positive since
F
is positive (
Lemma 22
), and
F\bigl({\sqrt{M_{j}}}\bigr)^{\dagger}F\bigl({\sqrt{M_{j}}}\bigr)=F\bigl({\sqrt{M_{j}}^{\dagger}\sqrt{M_{j}}}\bigr)=F(M_{j})
.
