Thus
F(\sqrt{M_{j}})=\sqrt{F(M_{j})}
.
We have

\bigl\lVert{\textstyle\sqrt{F(M_{j})}\psi}\bigr\rVert=\bigl\lVert{{F(\sqrt{M_{j}})}\psi}\bigr\rVert\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\bigl\lVert{F(\sqrt{M_{j}}\psi_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F(\psi_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F(\psi_{n})}\bigr\rVert\\
\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\lVert{\sqrt{M_{j}}\psi_{1}}\rVert\cdot\underbrace{\lVert{\psi_{2}}\rVert\cdots\lVert{\psi_{n}}\rVert}_{{}=1}=\lVert{\sqrt{M_{j}}\psi_{1}}\rVert.

Here
(*)
is by
item viii
and the definition of
\psi
.
And
(**)
is by
item i
.
This shows (
iii
).

We show (
iv
).
By
item iii
, without loss of generality, we can assume
k=1
.
By definition, the probability of outcome
j
using
M
given
\rho_{1}
is
\operatorname{tr}{M_{j}\rho_{1}}
.
And the probability of outcome
j
using
F({M})
given
\rho
is
\operatorname{tr}F(M_{j})\rho
.
So we have to show
\operatorname{tr}{M_{j}\rho_{1}}=\operatorname{tr}F(M_{j})\rho
.
We have

\operatorname{tr}F(M_{j})\rho\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\operatorname{tr}\Bigl({F(M_{j}\rho_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F(\rho_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F(\rho_{n})}\Bigr)\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(**)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\operatorname{tr}{M_{j}\rho_{1}}\cdot\underbrace{\operatorname{tr}\rho_{2}\cdots\operatorname{tr}\rho_{n}}_{{}=1}=\operatorname{tr}{M_{j}\rho_{1}}.

Here
(*)
is by
item iv
and the definition of
\rho
.
And
(**)
is by
item ii
.
This shows (
iv
).

We show (
v
).
By definition, the outcome probability in the POVM case is
\lVert{\sqrt{F(M_{j})}\psi}\rVert^{2}
or
\operatorname{tr}F(M_{j})\rho
, respectively.
And the probability in the projective measurement case is
\lVert{F(M_{j})\psi}\rVert^{2}
or
\operatorname{tr}F(M_{j})\rho F(M_{j})^{\dagger}
, respectively.
Since
M_{j}
is a projector, so is
F(M_{j})
by
item iii
.
Thus
F(M_{j})=F(M_{j})^{\dagger}=\sqrt{F(M_{j})}=F(M_{j})^{\dagger}F(M_{j})
.
Thus
\lVert{\sqrt{F(M_{j})}\psi}\rVert^{2}=\lVert{{F(M_{j})}\psi}\rVert^{2}
(i.e., the outcome probabilities for pure states are equal).
And
\operatorname{tr}F(M_{j})\rho=\operatorname{tr}F(M_{j})^{\dagger}F(M_{j})\rho\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}\operatorname{tr}F(M_{j})\rho F(M_{j})^{\dagger}
(i.e., the outcome probabilities for mixed states are equal).
Here
(*)
uses the circularity of the trace (
item ii
).
∎

General measurements,
Lemma 12

Proof.

We show (
i
).
Since
M
is a general measurement,
\sum_{i}M_{i}^{\dagger}M_{i}=1
.
By definition,
F({M})=\{{F(M_{i})}\}_{i}
.
We have

\sum_{i}F(M_{i})^{\dagger}F(M_{i})=\sum_{i}F(M_{i}^{\dagger}M_{i})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F\Bigl({\sum_{i}M_{i}^{\dagger}M_{i}}\Bigr)=F(1)=1.

Here
(*)
is by
Lemma 31
.
This shows (
i
).

We show (
ii
).
By definition
F({M})=\{F(M_{i})\}_{i}
.
By definition of non-normalized post-measurement states, we have
\psi_{1}^{\prime}=M_{j}\psi_{1}
and
\psi^{\prime}=F(M_{1})\psi
.
Then

\psi^{\prime}=F(M_{1})\psi\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F(M_{1}\psi_{1})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F(\psi_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F(\psi_{n})=F(\psi_{1}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F(\psi_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{p}}F(\psi_{n}).

Here
(*)
is by
item viii
and the definition of
\psi
.
This shows (
ii
).

We show (
iii
).
By definition
F({M})=\{F(M_{i})\}_{i}
.
By definition of non-normalized post-measurement states, we have
\rho_{1}^{\prime}=M_{j}\rho_{1}M_{j}^{\dagger}
and
\rho^{\prime}=F(M_{1})\rho F(M_{j})^{\dagger}
.
Then

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime}

\displaystyle=F(M_{1})\rho F(M_{1})^{\dagger}=F(M_{1})\rho F(M_{1}^{\dagger})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\hskip-72.26999pt\text{\tiny$(*)$}\hskip-72.26999pt}}{{=}}F(M_{1}\rho_{1}M_{1}^{\dagger})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F(\rho_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F(\rho_{n})

\displaystyle=F(\rho_{1}^{\prime})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F(\rho_{2})\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}\dots\otimes_{\mathfrak{m}}F(\rho_{n}).

Here
(*)
is by
item iv
and the definition of
\rho
.
This shows (
iii
).

We show (
iv
).
By definition of the “corresponding POVM”,
M^{\prime}=\{{M_{i}^{\dagger}M_{i}}\}_{i}
.
And
F(M)=\{{F(M_{i})}\}_{i}
and
F(M^{\prime})=\{{F(M_{i}^{\dagger}M_{i})}\}
.
Thus
F(M^{\prime})=\{{F(M_{i}^{\dagger}M_{i})}\}=\{{F(M_{i})^{\dagger}F(M_{i})}\}
which is the POVM corresponding to
F(M)
.
This shows (
iv
).
∎
